   1. Which of the following statements is true?
      my; oelafjk l=uk j.ka;sh i;H fõ o?

          1) The first generation of computers were built using transistors.
             m<uq mrïmrdfõ mß.Kl idok ,oafoa g%dkaisiag¾ Ndú; lrñks'
          2) Electronic Numerical Integrator And Computer (ENIAC) is a second generation computer.
             Electronic Numerical Integrator And Computer (ENIAC) hkq fojk mrïmrdfõ mß.Klhls'
          3) Ada Lovelace is the inventor of the Analytical Engine.
             Analytical Engine kï jQ hka;%fha ksuejqïlre jkafka wevd ,õf,aia (Ada Lovelace) h'
          4) Alan Turing is considered as the first computer programmer.
             m%:u mß.Kl l%uf,aLlhd (Programmer) f,i i,lkqfha we,ka áhqßka (Alan Turing) h'
          5) The first calculating device is believed to be the Abacus.

          Answer: 5)

          Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

          Option/ úl,amh 1)
          The first generation of computers (approximately 1940s to 1956) used vacuum tubes, not transistors.
          Transistors replaced vacuum tubes in the second generation of computers (1956–1963), offering better
          performance and reduced heat. Vacuum tubes were bulky and less efficient.
          m<uq mrïmrdfõ mß.Kl ^wdikak jYfhka 1940 isg 1956 olajd& g%dkaisiagr fkdj ßla; k, Ndú;d
          lf<ah' fojk mrïmrdfõ mß.Klj, ^1956–1963& g%dkaisiagr ßla; k, m%;sia:dmkh l< w;r

          úh'

          Option/ úl,amh 2)
          ENIAC, developed in the mid-1940s, is classified as a first generation computer. It used vacuum tubes
          and was one of the earliest general-purpose electronic digital computers. It consumed a lot of power and
          produced heat. ENIAC laid the groundwork for future advancements in computing.
          1940 .Kkaj, ueo Nd.fha ixj¾Okh lrk ,o ENIAC m<uq mrïmrdfõ mß.Klhla f,i
          j¾.SlrKh lr we;' th ßla; k, Ndú;d l< w;r uq,au fmdÿ ld¾h bf,lafg%dksl äðg,a mß.Kl
          j,ska tlls' th úYd, n,hla mßfNdackh lr ;dmh ksmoùh' mß.KllrKfha wkd.; ÈhqKqj i|yd
          ENIAC wä;d,u oeóh'

          Option/ úl,amh 3)
          Ada Lovelace is not the inventor of the Analytical Engine; that credit goes to Charles Babbage.
          However, Ada Lovelace is widely known for writing the first algorithm intended to be processed by such
          a machine, making her the first computer programmer. She worked closely with Babbage and deeply
          understood the machine's potential.
          Ada Lovelace úYaf,aIKd;aul tkaðfï kj ksmehqïlre fkdfõ. tu f.!rjh pd,aia nefíÊg ysñfõ'
          flfia fj;;a" tjeks hka;%hla uÕska ieliSug woyia lrk m<uq we,af.dß;u ,sùu i|yd wevd
          ,õf,aia mq¿,a f,i m%isoaOh" weh m<uq mß.Kl l%uf,aLlhd njg m;a l<dh' weh nefíÊ iuÕ

    Option/ úl,amh 4)
    While Alan Turing was a pioneer in computer science and theoretical computation, the title of the first
    computer programmer is generally given to Ada Lovelace for her work on the Analytical Engine in the
    1800s. Turing's contributions came much later and were more focused on computational theory and
    machine intelligence.
    Alan Turing mß.Kl úoHdfõ iy kHdhsl .Kkh lsÍfï mqfrda.dñfhl= jQ w;r" m<uq mß.Kl
    l%uf,aLljßh hk moúh idudkHfhka 1800 .Kkaj, úYaf,aIK tkaðu ms<sn| wehf.a ld¾hh i|yd
    Ada Lovelace fj; ,nd § we;' Turingg f.a odhl;ajhka fndfyda l,lg miqj meñKs w;r mß.Kl
    kHdh iy hka;% nqoaêh flfrys jeä wjOdkhla fhduq lrk ,§'

    Option/ úl,amh 5)
    The abacus is one of the earliest known tools used for calculation, dating back to ancient Mesopotamia
    and China. It was used to perform basic arithmetic operations long before the invention of mechanical or
    electronic computers. The abacus is still used in some parts of the world today for teaching arithmetic.
    wenlih hkq mqrdK fuifmdf;añhdfõ iy Ökfha isg .Kkh lsÍu i|yd Ndú;d lrk ,o merKd;u

 2. Which of the followings is a component of a Central Processing Unit (CPU)?
    uOHu ieliqï tallfha (CPU) wx.hla jkafka my; i|yka l=ula o?

    1) ROM                                   2) RAM                                    3) ALU
    4) L3 Cache memory                       5) Power supply unit

    Answer: 3)

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    Option/ úl,amh 1)
    ROM is not a CPU component, but rather a type of memory located on the motherboard. It stores
    firmware or permanent instructions needed to boot the computer. While essential for the computer’s
    operation, it is external to the CPU itself. Therefore, it does not qualify as a CPU component.
    ROM hkq CPU ys ixrplhla fkdj" ujq mqjrefõ msysgd we;s u;l j¾.hls' th mß.Klh wdrïN
    lsÍug wjYH iaÓrdx. fyda iaÓr Wmfoia .nvd lrhs' mß.Klfha l%shdldß;ajhg w;HjYH jqjo" th
    CPU gu ndysr fõ' tneúka" th CPU ixrplhla f,i iqÿiqlï fkd,nhs'

    Option/ úl,amh 2)
    RAM is not a part of the CPU, but a separate memory component used for temporary data storage
    while programs are running. It helps the CPU process tasks quickly by storing active data and instructions.
    However, RAM is located outside the CPU on the motherboard. Hence, it’s not considered a component
    of the CPU.
    RAM hkq CPU ys fldgila fkdfõ" kuq;a jevigyka l%shd;aul jk w;r;=r ;djld,sl o;a; .nvd
    lsÍu i|yd Ndú;d lrk fjku u;l ixrplhls' th l%shdldÍ o;a; iy Wmfoia .nvd lsÍfuka CPU
    g ld¾hhka blaukska ieliSug WmldÍ fõ' flfia fj;;a" RAM ujq mqjrefõ CPU j,ska msg; msysgd
    we;' tneúka" th CPU ys ixrplhla f,i fkdie,fla'

    Option/ úl,amh 3)
    This is the correct answer. The ALU is a core CPU component responsible for performing
    mathematical (arithmetic) and logical operations. It processes data and instructions retrieved from
    memory. Together with the Control Unit (CU) and registers, the ALU forms the heart of the CPU.
    fuh ksjerÈ ms<s;=rhs' ALU hkq .Ks;uh ^wxl .Ks;& iy ;d¾lsl fufyhqï isÿ lsÍu i|yd j.lsj
    hq;= CPU ys uQ,sl wx.hls' th u;lfhka ,nd.;a o;a; iy Wmfoia ilihs' md,k tAllh CU iy
    ,shdmÈxÑ lsÍï frcsia;r iuÕ tlaj" ALU , CPU ys yoj; idohs'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -3-                                          #ictfromabc


    Option/ úl,amh 4)
    L3 cache is a type of memory that helps improve CPU performance by storing frequently accessed data
    closer to the processor. While it is located near or even within the CPU chip, it is considered supporting
    memory, not a processing component. So, while it's associated with the CPU, it is not part of its core
    architecture like the ALU or Control Unit.
    L3 ksys; u;lh hkq ks;r m%fõY jk o;a; ilikhg iómj .nvd lsÍfuka CPU ld¾h idOkh
    jeä ÈhqKq lsÍug WmldÍ jk u;l j¾.hls' th CPU Ñmh wi, fyda we;=<; msysgd ;snqKo" th
    ieliqï ixrplhla fkdj" u;lhg iydh ùu f,i ie,fla' tneúka th CPU iuÕ iïnkaO ù ;snqKo"
    th ALU fyda md,k tAllh fuka tys uQ,sl wdlD;sfha fldgila fkdfõ'

    Option/ úl,amh 5)
    The Power Supply Unit provides the electrical power needed for the computer and its components to
    operate. It is a separate hardware unit and is not physically or functionally a part of the CPU. Its job
    is to convert electricity from an outlet into usable power for internal parts, including the CPU,
    motherboard, and storage devices.
    cj iemhqï tAllh mß.Klhg iy tys ixrpl l%shd;aul ùug wjYH úÿ,s n,h imhhs' th fjku
    oDvdx. tAllhla jk w;r fN!;slj fyda l%shdldÍj CPU ys fldgila fkdfõ' tys ld¾hh jkafka
    wf,úie,lska ,efnk úÿ,sh CPU ujq mqjrej iy .nvd Wmdx. we;=¿ wNHka;r fldgia i|yd Ndú;d
    l< yels n,hla njg mßj¾;kh lsÍuhs'

    Therefore, option 3) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 3) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 3. Which of the following binary numbers is equivalent to 10910?
    10910 g ;=,H oaúuh ixLHdj l=ula o?

    1) 11001002         2) 11011012         3) 10011012       4) 11010012         5) 11011002

    Answer: 2)

    Let’s Convert the Decimal number into Binary
    fuu oYu wxlh oaúuh njg mßj¾;kh lruq

    2     109
          54    -1
    2

    2     27 - 0

    2     13 - 1

    2     6     - 1

    2     3     - 0

          1     - 1


    So 10910 = 11011012

    Therefore, option 2) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 2) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 4. Consider the following description/definition list, rendered by a web browser:
    fjí w;ßlaiqjla (web browser) u.ska úoeyq (render) lrk ,o my; olajd we;s úia;r lsÍï$w¾:
    oelaùï (description/definition) ,ehsia;=j i,lkak(

        Zigzag
            Moving with sharp turns.
        Back and forth
            Moving side to side.
        Round and round
            Moving in a circle.

    Which of the following answers contains all the HTML tags required to create the above list?
    by; ,ehsia;=j ks¾udKh lsÍu i|yd wjYH ish¨ u HTML Wiq,k (tags) wvx.= jkafka my;
    i|yka ljr ms<s;=rl o?

    1) <dl>, <dt> only                        2) <ul>, <li> only                        3) <ol>, <li> only
    4) <dl>, <li> only                        5) <dl>, <dt>, <dd> only

    Answer: 5)

    The list shown in the question is a description list, where,
    m%Yakfha oelafjk ,ehsia;=j úia;r ,ehsia;=jls" tys§"

    •     Each term (like "Zigzag", "Back and forth") is a description term.
          iEu mohlau ^"Zigzag", "Back and forth" jeks& description mohls'
    •     Each definition (like "Moving with sharp turns.") is a description detail.
          iEu ks¾jpkhlau ^"Moving with sharp turns." jeks& description úia;rhls'

    In HTML, description lists are created using the following tags
    HTML ys" úia;r ,ehsia;= ks¾udKh lrkq ,nkafka my; Wiq,k Ndú;d lrñks

    •     <dl> – Description List (container tag) / úia;r ,ehsia;=j
    •     <dt> – Description Term / úia;r moh
    •     <dd> – Description Detail

    Since this is just a list of details, only <dl>, <dt>, and <dd> are used. This does not require the <ol> and
    <li> elements used in regular lists.
    fuys§ úia;r ,ehsia;=jla muKla olajd we;s neúka <dl>, <dt> iy <dd> muKla Ndú; fõ' idudkH
    ,ehsia;=j, Ndú; jk <ol>, <ul> , <li> jeks foa fuhg wjYH fkdfõ'

    Therefore, according to the answers below, the correct answer is the one that contains <dl>, <dt>, <dd>.
    tu ksid my; ms,s;=re j,g wkqj ksjerÈ ms<s;=r jkafka <dl>, <dt>, <dd> mj;sk ms<s;=rhs'

    Therefore, option 5) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 5) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 5. The content stored in .................... is read by using the optical technology.
    .......................................... l .nvd fldg we;s oE m%ldY ;dlaIKh Ndú; lrñka lshjkq ,efí'

    Which of the following answers is the most appropriate to fill the blank in the above statement?
    by; j.ka;sfha ysia;ek msrùu i|yd jvd;a iqÿiq jkafka my; i|yka ljrla o?

    1) Flash memory / iefk,s u;lh                    2) Floppy disk / kuH äialh
    3) Magnetic tape / pqïnl máh                     4) Compact disc / iqixys; äialh
    5) Hard disk / oDV äialh

    Answer: 4)

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    Option/ úl,amh 1)
    Flash memory is a type of solid-state memory used in USB drives, SSDs, and memory cards. It uses
    electrical charges to store data and does not rely on optical technology for reading or writing. It's known
    for its speed and durability.
    iefk,s u;lh hkq USB Odjl" SSD iy u;l ldâm;a j, Ndú;d jk >k;;aj u;l j¾.hls' th
    o;a; .nvd lsÍu i|yd úoHq;a wdfrdamK Ndú;d lrk w;r lshùu fyda ,sùu i|yd oDYH ;dlaIKh
    u; r|d fkdmj;S' th tys fõ.h iy l,ameje;au i|yd m%isoaOh'

    Option/ úl,amh 2)
    A floppy disk is a magnetic storage device used in older computers. Data is written and read using a
    magnetic read/write head, not optical methods. It was popular in the 1980s and 1990s but is now
    obsolete. This storage method does not involve optical technology.
    kuH äialhla hkq merKs mß.Klj, Ndú;d jk pqïNl .nvd Wmdx.hls' o;a; ,sùu iy lshùu isÿ
    lrkq ,nkafka oDYH l%u fkdj pqïNl lshùu$,sùfï ysila Ndú;d lrñks' th 1980 iy 1990 .Kkaj,
    ckm%sh jQ kuq;a oeka h,a mek f.dia we;' fuu .nvd l%uhg oDYH ;dlaIKh we;=<;a fkdfõ'

    Option/ úl,amh 3)
    Magnetic tape stores data using a magnetic coating and is typically used for long-term backups and
    archival storage. It is read sequentially using magnetic heads, not optical lasers. Like floppy disks,
    magnetic tape belongs to magnetic storage technology.
    pqïNl má pqïNl wdf,amkhla Ndú;fhka o;a; .nvd lrk w;r idudkHfhka È.= ld,Sk Wmia: iy
    f,aLkd.dr .nvd lsÍu i|yd Ndú;d fõ' th oDYH f,ai¾ fkdj pqïNl ysia Ndú;fhka wkql%ñlj
    lshjkq ,efí' kuH ;eá fuka" pqïNl má pqïNl .nvd ;dlaIKhg wh;a fõ'

    Option/ úl,amh 4)
    Compact Discs (CDs) store data that is read using optical technology—specifically, a laser beam that
    scans the disc surface to read data. Optical drives (like CD/DVD drives) interpret the reflections from the
    disc's surface. CDs are a classic example of optical storage.
    iqixys; ;eá (CD) oDYH ;dlaIKh Ndú;fhka lshjk o;a; .nvd lrhs - úfYaIfhka" o;a; lshùu
    i|yd ;eá u;=msg ialEka lrk f,ai¾ loïNhls' oDYH Odjl ^CD/DVD Odjl jeks& ;eáfha u;=msáka
    ,efnk mrdj¾;k w¾: ksrEmKh lrhs' CD hkq oDYH .nvd lsÍu i|yd iïNdjH WodyrKhls

    Option/ úl,amh 5)
    A hard disk (HDD) is a magnetic storage device that uses spinning platters and read/write heads to access
    data. It is not based on optical technology. While very common in computers, it falls under the category
    of magnetic storage, not optical.
    oDV ;eáhla ^HDD& hkq o;a; fj; m%fõY ùu i|yd N%uKh jk ;eá iy lshùfï$,sùfï ysia Ndú;d
    lrk pqïNl .nvd Wmdx.hls' th oDYH ;dlaIKh u; mokï fkdfõ' mß.Klj, b;d iq,N jqjo"
    th oDYH fkdj pqïNl .nvd ldKavhg wh;a fõ'

    Therefore, option 4) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 4) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 6. In modern computers, multiple levels of cache memory is used to optimize the performance. Among
    them, the ............ cache which is on/in ........... is the fastest and most expensive cache memory.
    kùk mß.Klj, ld¾h idOkh m%Yia; lsÍu i|yd nyqúO uÜgïj, ksys; (cache) u;l fhdod.kq
    ,efí' fïjd w;=frka ......................................... ;=<$u; mj;sk ......................................... ksys;
    u;lh fõ.j;a u iy ñ, wêl u ksys; u;lh fõ'
     Which of the following terms are correct to fill the blanks respectively in the above statement?
     by; jelsfha ysia;eka msrùu i|yd ksjerÈ mo wkqms<sfj<ska oelafjk ms<s;=r l=ula o?

     1) Level 1 (L1), main memory / m%Odk u;lh" m<uq uÜgfï (L1)
     2) Level 3 (L3), motherboard / uõ mqprej" f;jk uÜgfï (L3)
     3) Level 1 (L1), microprocessor / laIqø ilikh" m<uq uÜgfï (L1)
     4) Level 2 (L2), microprocessor / laIqø ilikh" fojk uÜgfï (L2)
     5) Level 3 (L3), microprocessor / laIqø ilikh" f;jk uÜgfï (L3)

     Answer: 3)

     Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

     Option/ úl,amh 1)
     While Level 1 (L1) cache is indeed the fastest, it is not located in the main memory. Main memory
     refers to RAM, which is separate from the processor and is much slower than cache. L1 cache is
     placed inside the CPU core itself.
     1 jk uÜgfï L1 ksys; u;lh ienúkau fõ.j;au jqj;a" th m%Odk u;lfha msysgd fkdue;' m%Odk
     u;lh RAM hkafkka woyia lrk w;r th ilikfhka fjkaj we;s w;r th ksys; u;lh jvd
     fnfyúka ukao.dó fõ' L1 ksys; u;lh we;a; jYfhkau CPU yrh ;=<u ;nd we;'

     Option/ úl,amh 2)
     Level 3 (L3) cache is typically slower than L1 and L2, and though it is often shared across multiple
     cores, it is still located on the CPU chip, not directly on the motherboard. The motherboard houses
     components, but cache memory is integrated into the processor architecture.
     3 jk uÜgfï (L3) ksys; u;lh idudkHfhka L1 iy L2 g jvd ukao.dó jk w;r" th fndfyda
     úg nyq yrhka yryd fnod .;a;o" th ;ju;a ujq mqjrefõ iDcqju fkdj CPU Ñmfha msysgd we;'
     ujq mqjrefõ ixrpl we;" kuq;a ksys; u;lh ilik wdlD;sh tAldnoaO lr we;'

     Option/ úl,amh 3)
     Level 1 (L1) cache is the fastest and most expensive type of cache memory and is placed directly
     within the microprocessor core. It stores small amounts of data and instructions that the processor uses
     frequently. Due to its speed and proximity to the CPU, it greatly enhances performance. However, it is
     also expensive to produce and has limited capacity.
     1 jk uÜgfï L1 ksys; u;lh fõ.j;au iy ñ, wêlu ksys; u;lh jk w;r th iDcqju laIqø
     ilik yrh ;=< ;nd we;' th ilikh ks;r Ndú;d lrk l=vd m%udKfha o;a; iy Wmfoia .nvd
     lrhs' tys fõ.h iy CPU fj; ióm ùu ksid" th ld¾h idOkh fnfyúka jeä ÈhqKq lrhs'
     flfia fj;;a" th ksIamdokh lsÍug o ñ, wêl jk w;r iSñ; Odß;djla we;'

     Option/ úl,amh 4)
     Level 2 (L2) cache is usually also located within or very close to the microprocessor. However, it is
     slower and cheaper than L1 cache, making it unsuitable as the “fastest and most expensive” cache
     mentioned in the question. L2 is still faster than main memory, but is not the top-performing cache level.
     2 jk uÜgfï L2 ksys; u;lh idudkHfhka laIqø ilikh ;=< fyda b;d wdikakj msysgd we;'
     flfia fj;;a" th L1 ksys; u;lhg jvd ukao.dó iy ,dNodhS fõ' L2 ;ju;a m%Odk u;lhg jvd
     fõ.j;a kuq;a by<u l%shdldÍ ksys; u;l uÜgu fkdfõ'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                    -7-                                #ictfromabc


    Option/ úl,amh 5)
    While L3 cache may be located on the same processor chip and shared across cores, it is slower than
    both L1 and L2. It is used to improve overall system performance and reduce bottlenecks but does not
    match L1 in speed or cost.
    L3 ksys; u;lh tlu ilik Ñmfha msysgd we;s w;r uOH yryd fnod .; yels jqjo" th L1
    iy L2 folgu jvd ukao.dó fõ' th iuia; moaO;s l%shdldß;ajh jeä ÈhqKq lsÍug iy ndOl wvq
    lsÍug Ndú;d lrhs" kuq;a fõ.h fyda msßjeh wkqj L1 g fkd.e,fma'

    Therefore, option 3) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 3) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 7. 10116 + 1108 =

    1) 42910            2) 101110             3) 32910         4) 52910       5) 13710

    Answer: 3)

    First Let’s Convert 10116 into Decimal
    uq,skau wms 10116 oYuhg mßj¾;kh lruq

          1              0              1
        × 162          × 161          × 160
         256             0              1
                 256 + 0 + 1 = 257
                   10116 = 257 10

    Now Let’s Convert 1108 into Decimal
    oeka wms 1108 oYuhg mßj¾;kh lruq

          1             1               0
         × 82         × 81             × 80
          64            8               0
                 64 + 8 + 0 = 72
                   1108= 72 10

    Now Let’s Add the two Decimal Numbers
    oeka wms oYu wxl fol tl;= lruq

    257 10 + 72 10 = 32910

    Therefore, option 3) is the correct answer.

 8. In an operating system, suspending the currently executing process and then resuming or starting
    another process is termed as
    fufyhqï moaO;shla ;=< oekg l%shd;aul fjñka mj;sk l%shdj,shla (process) w;aysgqjd (suspend)" bka
    miq" th h<s mgka .ekSu (resuming) fyda fjk;a l%shdj,shla mgka .ekSu (starting) y÷kajkq ,nkafka,

    1) paging / msgq lsÍu f,i h'             2) context switching / ikao¾N yqjudrej f,i h'
    3) swapping / m%;syrKh f,i h'            4) interrupting / w;=re ì£u f,i h'
    5) blocking /wjysr lsÍu f,i h'

    Answer: 2)

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    Option/ úl,amh 1)
    Paging is a memory management technique used by operating systems to divide the physical memory
    (RAM) and a program’s logical memory into fixed-size blocks called pages and frames. When a program
    runs, its pages are loaded into available frames in memory, not necessarily in a continuous order.
    msgq.; lsÍu hkq fN!;sl u;lh (RAM) iy jevigykl ;d¾lsl u;lh msgq iy rduq f,i ye¢kafjk
    ia:djr m%udKfha fldgia j,g fn§ug fufyhqï moaO;s úiska Ndú;d lrk u;l l<ukdlrK
    ;dlaIKhlss' jevigykla l%shd;aul jk úg" tys msgq u;lfha we;s rduq j,g mgjkq ,efí"
    wksjd¾hfhkau wLKav wkqms<sfj,lg fkdfõ'
    This helps efficiently use memory, avoids fragmentation, and allows processes to use more memory than
    physically available by using disk space as virtual memory.
    fuh u;lh ld¾hlaIuj Ndú;d lsÍug WmldÍ fõ" LKavkh ùu j<lajhs" iy ;eá wjldYh
    w:;H u;lh f,i Ndú;d lsÍfuka l%shdj,Skag fN!;slj ,nd .; yels m%udKhg jvd jeä u;lhla
    Ndú;d lsÍug bv i,ihs'
    Option/ úl,amh 2)
    Context switching in an operating system is the process of saving the current state of a running process
    so that it can be paused and resumed later, and then loading the state of another process to start or
    continue its execution. This allows the CPU to switch between multiple processes quickly, enabling
    multitasking and making it seem like all programs are running at the same time.
    fufyhqï moaO;shl ikao¾N iqúpkh hkq oekg l%shd;aul jk l%shdj,shl ;;ajh^ikao¾Nh&
    .nvdlr tu l%shdj,sh ;djld,slj kj;d miqj kej; wdrïN l< yels jk f,i th kj;d f.k
    th;a iu.u th fjk;a l%shdj,shl l%shdhkh wdrïN lsÍu fyda l<ska wjika l< ;;ajfha isg
    l%shd;aul lsÍu isÿ lsÍuhss' fuh CPU yg l%shdhk lsysmhla w;r fõ.fhka yqjudre ùug WmldÍ
    jk w;r nyq ld¾hhg bv i,id l%shdhk lsysmhla tljr CPU ;=< l%shd;aul jk f,i fmkajhs'

    Option/ úl,amh 3)
    Swapping is a memory management technique where a process is temporarily moved from the main
    memory (RAM) to the hard disk (usually to a space called the swap area) to free up memory for other
    processes. Later, the process can be brought back into RAM to continue execution. This allows the system
    to handle more processes than the available physical memory.
    m%;syrKh hkq u;l l<ukdlrK ;dlaIKhlss" tys§ l%shdj,shla ;djld,slj m%Odk u;lfha (RAM)
    isg oDV ;eáhg ^idudkHfhka m%;syrK m%foaYh f,i ye¢kafjk wjldYhlg& f.k hk w;r tuÕska
    wfkl=;a l%shdj,Ska i|yd u;lh ksoyia fõ' miqj" l%shdj,sh kej; RAM fj; f.k f.dia l%shd;aul
    lsÍu È.gu lrf.k hd yel' fuh moaO;shg mj;sk fN!;sl u;lhg jvd jeä bvla wjYH l%shdj,Ska
    yeisrùug bv i,ihs.
    Option/ úl,amh 4)
    An interrupt is a signal sent to the CPU by hardware or software to indicate that an event needs immediate
    attention. It temporarily stops the current process, saves its state, and allows the CPU to handle the
    interrupt (such as input from a keyboard or a timer alert). After handling it, the CPU resumes the original
    process. Interrupts help the system respond quickly to important tasks.
    w;=re ìÿula hkq isÿùulg laIKsl wjOdkhla wjYH nj oelaùug oDvdx. fyda uDÿldx.hla uÕska
    CPU fj; hjk ix{djls' th j;auka l%shdj,sh ;djld,slj kj;d" tys ;;ajh iqrlsk w;r" CPU
    yg ndOdj yeisrùug bv i,ihs ^h;=remqjrejlska wdodkh fyda ghsu¾ weÕùulska jeks&' th
    yeisrùfuka miq" CPU uq,a l%shdj,sh kej; wdrïN lrhs' ndOd lsÍï moaO;shg jeo.;a ld¾hhka i|yd
    blaukska m%;spdr oelaùug WmldÍ fõ'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                 -9-                                           #ictfromabc


     Option/ úl,amh 5)
     Blocking in operating systems refers to a situation where a process cannot continue until a specific
     condition is met or an event occurs. For example, if a process tries to read data from a file but the data
     isn’t available yet, it gets blocked - meaning it is paused and moved to a waiting state. The CPU then
     switches to another process. Once the required data becomes available, the blocked process is unblocked
     and can resume execution. This helps manage resources efficiently without wasting CPU time.
     fufyhqï moaO;sj, wjysr lsÍu hkq ksYaÑ; fldkafoaishla imqrd,k f;la fyda isÿùula isÿjk f;la
     l%shdj,shla È.gu lrf.k hd fkdyels ;;ajhls' WodyrKhla f,i" l%shdj,shla f.dkqjlska o;a;

     ,nd .; yels jQ miq" wjysr lrk ,o l%shdj,sh wjysr lsÍu bj;a lrkq ,nk w;r l%shd;aul lsÍu
     kej; wdrïN l< yelsh' fuh CPU ld,h kdia;s fkdlr iïm;a ld¾hlaIuj l<ukdlrKh lsÍug
     WmldÍ fõ'

 9. In modern operating systems, the .................... scheduler determines the transition of processes from
    the new state to the ready state.
    kùk fufyhqï moaO;sj, l%shdj,shla kj wjia:dfõ isg iQodkï wjia:djg jk ixl%dka;sh
    ................................ kshudlrKh u.ska ;SrKh lrkq ,efí.
     Which of the followings is the correct term to fill the blank in the above statement?
     by; j.ka;sfhys ysia;ek msrùu i|yd my; olajd we;s fhÿï w;=frka ksjerÈ jkafka ljrla o?

     1) mid-term / uOH ld,Sk                   2) long-term / È.= ld,Sk
     3) very long-term / b;d È.= ld,Sk         4) very short-term / b;d flá ld,Sk
     5) short-term / flá ld,Sk

     Answer: 2)
     Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

     Option/ úl,amh 1)
     The mid-term scheduler is responsible for temporarily removing processes from main memory and placing
     them in secondary storage (like a hard disk), a process called swapping. This helps manage memory usage
     and keeps the system responsive when many processes are running. It mainly deals with suspended
     processes rather than new or ready ones. Therefore, it does not control the transition from the new state to
     the ready state.
     uOH ld,Sk kshulrKh m%Odk u;lfhka l%shdj,s ;djld,slj bj;a lr oaú;shsl .nvdfõ ^oDV ;eáhla
     jeks& ;eîu i|yd j.lsj hq;=h" th m%;syrKh f,i ye¢kafjk l%shdj,shls' fuh u;l Ndú;h
     l<ukdlrKh lsÍug WmldÍ jk w;r fndfyda l%shdj,Ska l%shd;aul jk úg moaO;sh m%;spdrd;aulj
     ;nd .kS' th m%Odk jYfhka kj fyda iQodkï l%shdj,Ska fjkqjg w;aysgqjk ,o l%shdj,Ska iuÕ lghq;=
     lrhs' tneúka" th kj ;;ajfha isg iQodkï ;;ajhg udreùu md,kh fkdlrhs'

     Option/ úl,amh 2)
     The long-term scheduler, is the component that decides which new processes are allowed to enter the ready
     state from the new state. It controls how many processes are admitted into the system at a time, affecting
     the degree of multiprogramming.
     È.=ld,Sk kshulrKh" kj ;;ajfhka iQodkï ;;ajhg we;=¿ ùug wjir fok kj l%shdj,Ska ;SrKh
     lrk ixrplh fõ' th tljr moaO;shg fldmuK l%shdj,Ska we;=<;a lrkafkao hkak md,kh lrhs"
     nyq l%uf,aLk uÜgug n,mdhs'

     This scheduler works less frequently compared to others but plays a key role in managing overall system
     load. It is the correct scheduler for the given question.
     fuu kshulrKh wfkla tAjdg idfmalaIj wvqfjka l%shd lrhs" kuq;a iuia; moaO;s Ndrh l<ukdlrKh
     lsÍfï§ m%Odk ld¾hNdrhla bgq lrhs' § we;s m%Yakh i|yd ksjerÈ kshulrKh thhs'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -10-                                          #ictfromabc


    Option/ úl,amh 3)
    This term is not recognized in standard operating system terminology. There is no such component called
    a very long-term scheduler in modern operating systems. It may be a made-up or incorrect term and should
    be disregarded in this context.
    fuu moh iïu; fufyhqï moaO;s mdßNdIsl Yío ud,dfõ y÷kdf.k fkdue;' kùk fufyhqï
    moaO;sj, b;d È.=ld,Sk kshulrKh f,i ye¢kafjk tjeks ixrplhla fkdue;' th f.d;k ,o fyda
    jerÈ mohla úh yels w;r fuu ikao¾Nh ;=< th fkdi,ld yeßh hq;=h'

    Option/ úl,amh 4)
    Similar to the previous option, very short-term scheduler is not a standard or meaningful term in operating
    system design. It appears to be a mistaken or non-technical label, possibly intended to confuse or test
    understanding. It has no role in process state transitions.
    fmr úl,amhg iudkj" b;d flá ld,Sk kshulrKh hkq fufyhqï moaO;s ks¾udKfha iïu; fyda
    w¾:j;a fhÿula fkdfõ' th jerÈ fyda ;dlaIKsl fkdjk f,an,hla f,i fmfka" iuyr úg jHdl+,
    lsÍug fyda wjfndaOh mÍlaId lsÍug woyia lr we;' l%shdj,s ;;aj ixl%dka;s j,§ thg lsisÿ
    ld¾hNdrhla fkdue;'

    Option/ úl,amh 5)
    The short-term scheduler, also known as the CPU scheduler, is responsible for selecting one process
    from the ready queue and assigning it to the CPU for execution. It makes decisions frequently (every
    few milliseconds) and is critical to system responsiveness. However, it does not handle the transition
    from new to ready—that is the job of the long-term scheduler.
    flá ld,Sk kshulrKh" CPU kshulrKh f,io ye¢kafõ" iQodkï fmda,sfuka tla l%shdj,shla
    f;dardf.k th l%shd;aul lsÍu i|yd CPU fj; mejÍu i|yd j.lsj hq;=h' th ks;r ;SrK .kS
    ^iEu ñ,s ;;amr lsysmhlg jrla& iy moaO;s m%;spdr oelaùug b;d jeo.;a fõ' flfia fj;;a" th
    kj isg iQodkï olajd ixl%uKh yiqrejkafka ke; - th È.=ld,Sk kshulrKfhys ld¾hhhs'

    Therefore, option 2) is the correct answer.

 10. Consider the following statements:
     my; i|yka j.ka;s i,lkak(
    A. Plagiarism is a common threat to information systems.
       rpkd fp!r;ajh (Plagiarism) f;dr;=re moaO;sj,g we;s fmdÿ ;¾ckhls'
    B. Plagiarism means claiming someone else’s creation as one’s own.
       rpkd fp!r;ajfhka woyia jkafka fjk;a whl=f.a ks¾udKhla ;uqkaf.a hehs lshd mEuhs'
    C. Piracy is a synonym for plagiarism.
       m%ldYk fidrlu (Piracy) rpkd fp!r;ajh i|yd iudkd¾: mohls'
    Which of the above statements is/are correct?
    by; j.ka;s w;=frka ksjerÈ jkafka ljrla o?

    1) A only           2) B only           3) C only          4) A and B only 5) B and C only
       A muKs              B muKs              C muKs             A yd B muKs     B yd C muKs

    Answer: 2)

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    Statement/ m%ldYh A)
    Plagiarism is an ethical and legal issue, not a direct technical threat to an information system. It doesn’t
    harm the software, hardware, or network functionality of a system like viruses or hacking would. Instead,
    it affects the content or data stored in or processed by the system, particularly in academic or publishing
    environments. Therefore, it’s not accurate to call plagiarism a threat to information systems themselves.
    rpkd fp!r;ajh hkq f;dr;=re moaO;shlg iDcq ;dlaIKsl ;¾ckhla fkdj" iodpdrd;aul iy kS;suh
    .eg¨jls' th ffjria fyda wkjirfhka we;=¿ùu jeks moaO;shl uDÿldx." oDvdx. fyda cd,
    l%shdldß;ajhg ydks fkdlrhs' tA fjkqjg" th moaO;sh ;=< .nvd lr we;s fyda ilik ,o wka;¾.;hg
    fyda o;a; j,g n,mdhs" úfYaIfhka wOHhk fyda m%ldYk mßirj,' tneúka" rpkd fp!r;ajh hkq
    f;dr;=re moaO;sj,gu ;¾ckhla f,i ye¢kaùu ksjerÈ fkdfõ'

    Statement/ m%ldYh B)
    Plagiarism refers to presenting another person's work be it writing, ideas, music, code, or design as your
    own, without giving credit. This includes copying and pasting content from websites, submitting others'
    work as your own, or failing to cite sources. It is a serious offense in both educational and professional
    environments. Thus, this is a proper definition of plagiarism.
    rpkd fp!r;ajh hkq fjk;a mqoa.,fhl=f.a lD;shla th ,sùu" woyia" ix.S;h" fla;h fyda ks¾udKh
    fõjd f.!rjh ,nd fkd§ Tfíu f,i bÈßm;a lsÍuhs' fuhg fjí wvú j,ska wka;¾.;h msgm;a
    lr we,ùu" wka whf.a lD;s Tfíu f,i bÈßm;a lsÍu fyda uQ,dY% Wmqgd oelaùug wfmdfydi;a ùu
    we;=<;a fõ' wOHdmksl iy jD;a;Sh mßirhka foflysu th nrm;, jrols' fï wkqj" fuh rpkd
    fp!r;ajh ms<sn| ksis w¾: oelaùuls'

    Statement/ m%ldYh C)
    While piracy and plagiarism are both unethical, they are not synonyms. Piracy refers to the
    unauthorized use, copying, or distribution of copyrighted material, such as software, movies, or
    music. Plagiarism, on the other hand, is about misrepresenting someone else’s original work as your
    own.
    m%ldYk fidrlu iy rpkd fp!r;ajh hk folu iodpdr úfrdaë jqj;a" tAjd iudk mo fkdfõ' m%ldYk

    Tfíu f,i jerÈ f,i ksrEmKh lsÍuhs'

    Therefore, option 2) is the correct answer.

 11. Consider the following logic circuit implemented using universal gates:
     my; fmkajd we;s i¾j;% (universal) oajdr wdOdrfhka idod we;s ;d¾lsl mßm:h i,lkak(




    The above circuit is equivalent to a/an

    1)   NOT Gate / NOT oajdrhlg h'
    2)   AND Gate / AND oajdrhlg h'
    3)   OR Gate / OR oajdrhlg h'
    4)   NAND Gate / NAND oajdrhlg h'
    5)   NOR Gate / NOR oajdrhlg h'

    Answer: 2)

    This logic circuit, made up of universal gates (NOR), is equivalent to a basic logic gate used in digital
    logic design.
    id¾j;% oajdr (NOR) j,ska iukaú; fuu ;d¾lsl mßm:h" äðg,a ;d¾lsl ks¾udKfha Ndú;d jk
    uQ,sl ;d¾lsl oajdrhlg iudk fõ'

    Let’s build up the logical expression for the given logic circuit assuming the inputs are A and B.
    wdodk A iy B hehs Wml,amkh lrñka § we;s ;d¾lsl mßm:h i|yd ;d¾lsl m%ldYkh
    f.dvkÕuq'

    ̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅
     ̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅
    (A  + A) + (B  ̅̅̅̅̅̅̅
                      + B)

    Let’s simplify the given expression using Boolean laws.
    nQ,Sh kS;s Ndú;fhka § we;s m%ldYkh ir, lruq'

    ̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅
     ̅̅̅̅̅̅̅̅

                      + B)
    ̿̿̿̿̿̿̿̿̿̿ ̿̿̿̿̿̿̿̿̿̿

    (A + A). (B + B)          (Double complement law / oaú;aj m%;sf,dau kHdh)
    A. (B + B)                (Idempotent law / ;foajNdú kHdh)
    A. B                      (Idempotent law / ;foajNdú kHdh )

    The simplification shows that the given logic circuit is equivalent to an AND gate.
    ir, lsÍfuka fmkakqï lrkafka § we;s ;d¾lsl mßm:h AND oajdrhlg iudk njhs'

 12. “An analog signal is sampled at regular intervals and represented as 16 bit values.”
     "m%;siu ix{djla ksh; ld,dka;rj, oS ksheÈ lr ìgq 16 ys w.hka f,i ksrEmKh lrkq ,efí'"
    Which of the followings is best described by the above statement?
    by; j.ka;sh fyd¢ka u úia;r lrkq ,nkafka my; i|yka l=ulska o?

    1)   Amplitude Modulation / úia;dr uQ¾ckh (AM)
    2)   Frequency Modulation / ixLHd; uQ¾ckh (FM)
    3)   Pulse Code Modulation / iamkaÈ; fla; uQ¾ckh (PCM)
    4)   Phase Modulation / l,d uQ¾ckh (PM)
    5)   Pulse Width Modulation / iamkao ú;r uQ¾ckh (PWM)

    Answer: 3)

    Modulation is the process of varying one or more properties (such as its amplitude, frequency, or phase)
    of periodic waveform called the carrier signal to encode information for transmission over a
    communication channel.
    uqyq¾cKh hkq ikaksfõok kd,sldjla yryd iïfm%aIKh i|yd f;dr;=re fla;kh lsÍug jdyl ix{dj
    f,iska ye¢kafjk wdj¾;sl ;rx. wdlD;shl .=Kdx. tlla fyda lsysmhla ^tys úia;drh" ixLHd;h
    fyda l,dj jeks& fjkia lsÍfï l%shdj,shhs'

    Amplitude Modulation (AM) / úia;dr uQ¾ckh
    In amplitude modulation, the amplitude of the carrier signal is varied in proportion to the amplitude of the
    information (baseband) signal, while the frequency and phase of the carrier remain constant.
    úia;drh uqyq¾ckfha§" jdyl ix{dfõ úia;drh f;dr;=re ix{dfõ úia;drhg iudkqmd;slj fjkia
    lrk w;r" jdylfha ixLHd;h iy l,dj ksh;j mj;S'

    In AM, the louder the signal (for example, a voice or music), the higher the amplitude of the carrier wave,
    and the quieter the signal, the lower the amplitude.
    AM j,§" ix{dj ^WodyrKhla f,i" yvla fyda ix.S;hla& jeä jk ;rug" jdyl ;rx.fha úia;drh
    jeä jk w;r" ix{dj ksYaYío jk ;rug" úia;drh wvq fõ'

    Frequency modulation (FM) / ixLHd; uQ¾ckh
    In frequency modulation, the frequency of the carrier signal is varied in proportion to the amplitude of the
    information (baseband) signal, while the amplitude and phase of the carrier remain constant.
    ixLHd; uqyq¾ckfha§" jdyl ix{dfõ ixLHd;h f;dr;=re ix{dfõ úia;drhg iudkqmd;slj fjkia
    lrk w;r" jdylfha úia;drh iy l,dj ksh;j mj;S'

    The louder the information signal, the more the frequency of the carrier signal shifts.
    f;dr;=re ix{dfõ Yíoh jeä jk ;rug jdyl ix{dfõ ixLHd;h jeä fõ'

    Phase modulation (PM) / l,d uQ¾ckh
    In phase modulation, the phase of the carrier signal is varied in proportion to the amplitude of the
    information (baseband) signal, while the carrier’s amplitude and frequency remain constant.
    l,d uQ¾ckfha§" jdyl ix{dfõ l,dj f;dr;=re ix{dfõ úia;drhg iudkqmd;slj fjkia lrk w;r"
    jdylfha úia;drh iy ixLHd;h ksh;j mj;S'

    As the amplitude of the information signal changes, the phase of the carrier shifts accordingly.
    f;dr;=re ix{dfõ úia;drh fjkia jk úg" jdylfha l,dj tA wkqj fjkia fõ'

    Pulse Code Modulation (PCM) / iamkaok fla; uQ¾ckh
    Pulse Code Modulation encoding is a technique used to convert analog signals into digital signals.
    iamkaok fla; uQ¾ckh hkq m%;siu ix{d wxls; ix{d njg mßj¾;kh lsÍu i|yd Ndú;d lrk
    ;dlaIKhls'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                              -14-                                         #ictfromabc


    Pulse Width Modulation (PWM) / iamkao ú;r uQ¾ckh
    Pulse Width Modulation (PWM) is a technique used to control the power delivered to electrical devices
    by varying the width (duration) of digital pulses.
    iamkao ú;r uQ¾ckh hkq äðg,a iamkaokj, m<, ^ld,iSudj& fjkia lsÍfuka úoHq;a Wmdx.j,g ,nd
    fok n,h md,kh lsÍug Ndú;d lrk ;dlaIKhls'

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    The statement "An analog signal is sampled at regular intervals and represented as 16-bit values"
    perfectly describes Pulse Code Modulation (PCM)
    m%;siu ix{djla ks;sm;d ld, mr;rhkays§ idïm, ,ndf.k ìgq 16 w.hka f,i ksrEmKh flf¾
    hk m%ldYh iamkaok fla; uQ¾ckh mßmQ¾K f,i úia;r lrhs

    Sampling: The analog signal is measured at regular time intervals.
    kshe§u( m%;siu ix{dj ks;sm;d ld, mr;rhkays§ ukskq ,efí'

    Quantization: The amplitude of each sample is assigned a discrete value (e.g., using 16-bit binary).
    m%udKkh( tla tla idïm,fha úia;drhg úúla; w.hla mjrkq ,efí ^Wod( ìgq 16 oaúuh Ndú;d
    lsÍu&'

    Encoding: These values are converted into a binary format for digital transmission or storage.
    fla;kh( fuu w.hka äðg,a iïfm%aIKh fyda .nvd lsÍu i|yd oaúuh wdlD;shla njg mßj¾;kh
    fõ'

    This is exactly how PCM works — it's a digital representation of an analog signal.
    PCM l%shd lrk wdldrh fuhhs - th m%;siu ix{djl äðg,a ksrEmKhls'

    Why the other options are incorrect / wfkl=;a úl,am jerÈ jkafka wehs
    Option/ úl,amh 1)
    odifies the amplitude of a carrier wave in response to the analog signal.
    m%;siu ix{djg m%;spdr jYfhka jdyl ;rx.hl úia;drh fjkia lrhs'

    Does not involve sampling or digitization.
    kshe§u fyda äðg,alrKh we;=<;a fkdfõ'

    Option/ úl,amh 2)
    Modulates the frequency of a carrier signal.
    jdyl ix{djl ixLHd;h uQ¾ckh lrhs'

    Again, it's analog and doesn't use 16-bit samples.
    kej;;a" th m%;siu jk w;r ìgq 16 idïm, Ndú;d fkdlrhs'

    Option/ úl,amh 4)
    Changes the phase of the carrier wave in accordance with the signal.
    ix{djg wkql+,j jdyl ;rx.fha wÈhr fjkia lrhs'

    Also analog, not digital sampling.
    ;jo äðg,a idïm,lrKh fkdj m%;siuh'

    Option/ úl,amh 5)

    ix{d úia;drh ksrEmKh lsÍu i|yd iamkaokj, m<, fjkia lrhs'

    It's used more in power control and not equivalent to PCM’s 16-bit sampling method.
    th n, md,kfha§ jeämqr Ndú;d jk w;r PCM ys ìgq 16 idïm,lrK l%uhg iudk fkdfõ'

 13. Two machines with the IP addresses 192.248.16.30 and 192.248.16.90 are connected to a Local Area
     Network (LAN). Which of the followings is a suitable subnet mask for this network?
     IP ,smsk 192.248.16.30 iy 192.248.16.90 iys; hka;% folla ia:dkSh fmfoia cd,hlg (LAN)
     iïnkaO fldg we;' my; i|yka ljrla fuu cd,h i|yd iqÿiq Wmcd, wdjrKhla jkafka o?

    1) 192.255.255.255                2) 192.248.16.0                           3) 255.255.255.224
    4) 255.255.255.128                5) 255.255.255.255

    Answer: 4)

    To determine a suitable subnet mask, we must make sure both IP addresses are in the same subnet.
    iqÿiq Wmcd, wdjrKhla ;SrKh lsÍu i|yd" wms IP ,smsk folu tlu Wmcd,hl we;s njg j. n,d
    .; hq;=h'

    Given / ,nd § we;
    IP1: 192.248.16.30
    IP2: 192.248.16.90

    Let’s examine the binary forms of the last octet of each IP (since the first 3 octets are the same)
    tla tla IP ys wjidk wIaGufha oaúuh wdldr mÍlaId lruq ^m<uq wIaGu 3 iudk neúka&

    192.248.16.30 → Last octet in binary / oaúuh ;=< wjidk wIaGuh : 00011110
    192.248.16.90 → Last octet in binary / oaúuh ;=< wjidk wIaGuh : 01011010

    We need a subnet mask that groups both of these addresses into the same network.
    fuu ,smsk folu tlu cd,hlg ldKav lrk Wmcd, wdjrKhla wmg wjYH fõ'

    Option (4): 255.255.255.128

    This is a /25 subnet mask, which means:
    fuh /25 Wmcd, wdjrKhla" tkï(

    Network size = 2^7 = 128 IP addresses
    cd, m%udKh = 2^7 = 128 IP ,smsk

    Valid range: 192.248.16.0 to 192.248.16.127
    j,x.= mrdih( 192.248.16.0 isg 192.248.16.127 olajd

    Check if both IPs fall into this range
    IP folu fuu mrdihg jefÜoehs mÍlaId lruq

    192.248.16.30 within range / mrdih ;=<
    192.248.16.90 within range / mrdih ;=<

    So both are in the same subnet.
    tneúka folu tlu Wmcd,fha we;'

    Why other options are wrong / fjk;a úl,am jerÈ jkafka wehs
    Option/ úl,amh 1)
    Not a valid subnet mask (starts with 192 – invalid for a mask).
    j,x.= Wmcd, wdjrKhla fkdfõ ^192 ka wdrïN fõ - wdjrKhla i|yd j,x.= fkdfõ&'

    Option/ úl,amh 2)
    That’s a network address, not a subnet mask.
    th cd, ,smskhls" Wmcd, wdjrKhla fkdfõ'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -16-                                           #ictfromabc


    Option/ úl,amh 3)
    /27 → Range: 192.248.16.0 – 192.248.16.31
    /27 → mrdih( 192.248.16.0 – 192.248.16.31

    IP 192.248.16.90 not in range.
    IP 192.248.16.90 mrdifha fkdue;'

    Option/ úl,amh 5)

    /32 → ;ks i;aldrlhlg muKs" tneúka Wmdx. 2la i|yd j,x.= fkdfõ'

    Therefore, option 4) is the correct answer.

    internet. It allows businesses and consumers to conduct transactions online without the need for physical
    interaction.

    that e-commerce could be part of a larger e-business strategy.

 18. Which of the following is a correct IPv4 address?
     my; i|yka ljrla ksjerÈ IPv4 ,smskhla jkafka o?

    1) 192.248.0.0.1                  2) 192.258.2.1                              3) 8.8.8.8
    4) 10.256.8.9                     5) 255.255.255.268

    Answer: 3)

    IP addresses
    An IP address is a logical identifier assigned to a device when it connects to a network.
    IP ,smskhla hkq cd,hlg iïnkaO jk úg Wmdx.hlg mjrk ;d¾lsl y÷kd.ekSuls'

    It identifies the device at a higher level, such as within a larger network or the internet.
    th úYd, cd,hla fyda wka;¾cd,h jeks by< uÜgul§ Wmdx.h y÷kd .kS'

    Unlike MAC addresses, which are tied to hardware, IP addresses are logical and can change.
    oDVdx. iuÕ ne£ we;s MAC ,smsk fuka fkdj" IP ,smsk ;d¾lsl jk w;r fjkia úh yel'

    For instance, a device’s IP address may change if it connects to a different network.
    WodyrKhla f,i" Wmdx.hla fjkia cd,hlg iïnkaO jqjfyd;a tys IP ,smskh fjkia úh yel'

    Types of IP Addresses / IP ,smsk j¾.
    IPv4
    It uses a 32-bit number format, represented as four decimal numbers separated by dots (Ex. 192.168.1.1).
    th ìgq 32 ixLHd wdlD;shla Ndú;d lrhs" ;s;a j,ska fjka lrk ,o oYu ixLHd y;rla f,i
    ksrEmKh flf¾ ^Wod' 192.168.1.1&'

    This is called the dotted decimal notation.
    fuh ;s;a oYuh wxlkh f,i ye¢kafõ'

    IPv6
    This newer version uses a 128-bit address format, represented as eight groups of four hexadecimal digits
    separated by colons.
    fuu kj wkqjdoh ìgq 128ys ,smsk wdlD;shla Ndú;d lrhs" flda,ka j,ska fjka lrk ,o IâoYuh
    b,lalï y;rl ldKav wgla f,i ksrEmKh flf¾'
    2001:0db8:85a3:0000:0000:8a2e:0370:7334

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    An IPv4 address consists of four octets, each separated by a dot (.), and each octet must be a number from
    0 to 255.
    IPv4 ,smskhla ;s;lska (.) fjka lrk ,o wIagl y;rlska iukaú; jk w;r" iEu wIaglhlau 0 isg
    255 olajd wxlhla úh hq;=h'

    Let’s examine each option, / tla tla úl,amh mÍlaId lr n,uq"
    (1) 192.248.0.0.1
    Incorrect: This has 5 octets (too many). IPv4 must have only 4 parts.
    jerÈhs( fuhg wIaGu 5la we; ^b;d jeäh&' IPv4 ys fldgia 4la muKla ;sìh hq;=h'

    (2) 192.258.2.1
    Incorrect: The second octet is 258, which is greater than 255.
    jerÈhs( fojk wIaGuh 258 jk w;r th 255 g jvd jeäh'

    (3) 8.8.8.8
    Correct: All four octets are valid numbers between 0 and 255.
    ksjerÈhs( ish¨u wIaGu y;ru 0 iy 255 w;r j,x.= ixLHd fõ'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -21-                                           #ictfromabc


     This is a well-known public DNS server provided by Google.
     fuh .+.,a úiska imhk ,o m%isoaO fmdÿ DNS fiajdodhlhls'

     (4) 10.256.8.9
     Incorrect: The second octet is 256, which is outside the valid range.
     jerÈhs( fojk wIaGuh 256 jk w;r th j,x.= mrdifhka msg; fõ'

     (5) 255.255.255.268
     Incorrect: The last octet is 268, which is more than 255.
     jerÈhs( wjidk wIaGuh 268 jk w;r th 255 g jvd jeäh'

     Therefore, option 3) is the correct answer.
     tneúka" 3) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 19. User Datagram Protocol (UDP) is a ................. layer protocol.
     User Datagram Protocol (UDP) hkq .................................. ia;r kshudj,shls (layer protocol).

     Which of the following layers is the most suitable to fill the blank in the above statement?
     by; jelsfha ysia;ek msrùu i|yd my; ia;r w;=frka jvd;a iqÿiq jkafka l=ula o?

     1) physical         2) data link        3) network          4) transport       5) application
        fN!;sl              o;a; ikaOdk         cd,                 m%jdyk             fhÿï

     Answer: 4)

     OSI Model
     The OSI model, also known as the Open Systems Interconnection model, serves as a comprehensive and
     detailed conceptual framework designed to facilitate and enhance the understanding and implementation
     of network communications among computing systems.
     OSI wdlD;sh" újD; moaO;s wka;¾ iïnkaO;d wdlD;sh f,io ye¢kafõ" mß.Kl moaO;s w;r cd,

     Application layer / fhÿï ia:rh

    Session layer / ieis ia:rh
    In communication that takes place in networking, it is essential that there is a better mutual relationship
    between the parties in which it takes place. The reason is that otherwise the data communicated can be
    communicated to someone else.
    cd,lrKfha§ isÿjk ikaksfõokhla ;=< th isÿjk md¾Yaj w;r jvd fyd| wfkHdakH iïnkaOhla
    mej;Su w;HjYH fõ' Bg fya;=j tfia fkdjqjfyd;a ikaksfõokh lrkq ,nk o;a;h fjk;a whl=g
    jqjo ikaksfõokh ùug yels ùuhs'

    i|yd j.lsj hq;=h


    Transport layer / m%jdyk ia:rh

    olajd ikaksfõokh iemhSuhs'

    yd ld¾hlaIuj tys .ukdka;hg <Õd jk njg j. n,d .kS'

    Network layer / cd, ia:rh

    Physical layer / fN!;sl ia:rh

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    User Datagram Protocol (UDP) is a protocol used to send data without establishing a connection. It is
    known for its speed and simplicity, but does not guarantee delivery, ordering, or error checking like TCP
    does.
    User Datagram Protocol (UDP) hkq iïnkaO;djhla ia:dms; fkdlr o;a; heùug Ndú;d lrk
    fm%dfgdaflda,hls' th tys fõ.h iy ir, nj i|yd m%isoaOh" kuq;a TCP fuka fnod yeÍu" weKjqï
    lsÍu fyda fodaI mÍlaId lsÍu iy;sl fkdlrhs'

    It operates at the Transport Layer of the TCP/IP model (and the OSI model).
    th TCP/IP wdlD;sfha ^iy OSI wdlD;sfha& m%jdyk ia:rfha l%shd;aul fõ'

    Why the other options are incorrect / wfkl=;a úl,am jerÈ jkafka wehs
    Option/ úl,amh 1)
    Deals with hardware and transmission of raw bits — not protocols like UDP.
    oDvdx. iy wuq ìgq iïfm%aIKh iuÕ lghq;= lrhs - UDP jeks fm%dfgdaflda, fkdfõ'

    Option/ úl,amh 2)
    Responsible for node-to-node data transfer — protocols like Ethernet, not UDP.
    node-to-node o;a; yqjudrej i|yd j.lsj hq;=h - B;¾fkÜ jeks fm%dfgdaflda," UDP fkdfõ'

    Option/ úl,amh 3)
    Handles IP addressing and routing — UDP is built on top of IP, but not part of this layer.
    IP ,smsk iy ud¾..; lsÍu yiqrejhs - UDP IP u; f.dvk.d we;" kuq;a fuu ia:rfha fldgila
    fkdfõ'

    Option/ úl,amh 5)
    Includes protocols like HTTP, FTP — UDP supports application protocols but is not part of this layer.
    HTTP, FTP jeks fm%dfgdaflda, we;=<;a fõ - UDP fhÿï fm%dfgdaflda, i|yd iyh olajhs kuq;a fuu
    ia:rfha fldgila fkdfõ'

    Therefore, option 4) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 4) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

    Router / ud¾.lh
    A router is a key network device that manages and directs traffic between networks.
    ud¾.lhla cd, w;r .ukd.ukh l<ukdlrKh lrk iy fufyhjk m%Odk cd, Wmdx.hls'

    Inside a router, several essential components and devices work together to perform its functions.
    ud¾.lhla ;=<" tys ld¾hhka bgq lsÍu i|yd w;HjYH ixrpl iy Wmdx. lsysmhla tlg l%shd lrhs'

    A router is a computer and networking device that forwards data packets between computer networks,
    including internetworks such as the global Internet.
    ud¾.lh hkq f.da,Sh wka;¾cd,h jeks wka;¾cd, jev we;=¿j mß.Kl cd, w;r o;a; melÜ fhduq
    lrk mß.Kl iy cd,lrK Wmdx.hls'

    How Routers Work / ud¾.lhl l%shdldÍ;ajh
    When a data packet arrives at a router, the router examines the packet's header to identify its destination
    address.
    o;a; melÜgqjla rjqgrhlg meñKs úg" rjqgrh tys .ukdka; ,smskh y÷kd .ekSug melÜgqfõ
    YS¾Ih mÍlaId lrhs'

    Using a routing table, which contains information about available paths, the router determines the best
    next hop for the packet.
    mj;sk ud¾. ms<sn| f;dr;=re wvx.= ud¾..; lsÍfï j.=jla Ndú;d lrñka" rjqgrh melÜgqj i|yd
    fyd|u B<Õ fydma tl ;SrKh lrhs'

    This process is equal to a traveler consulting a map to find the best route to their destination.
    fuu l%shdj,sh ixpdrlfhl= ;u .ukdka;hg fyd|u ud¾.h fidhd .ekSug is;shula WmfoaYkh lsÍu
    yd iudk fõ'

    The router then forwards the packet to the next device or router along the path, continuing this process
    until the packet reaches its final destination.
    rjqgrh miqj melÜgqj B<Õ Wmdx.hg fyda ud¾.h Tiafia rjqgrhg fhduq lrhs" melÜgqj tys
    wjidk .ukdka;h lrd <Õd jk f;la fuu l%shdj,sh È.gu lrf.k hhs'

 21. Consider the following system implementation methods:
     my; olajd we;s moaO;s l%shdjg kexùfï l%u i,ld n,kak(

     A - Direct / f¾Çh               B – Phase / l,d          C – Parallel / iudka;r

    Which of the above methods can be recommended to implement an emergency call handling system?
    yÈis weu;=ï fufyhqï moaO;shla l%shdjg kexùu (implement) i|yd by; i|yka l%u w;=frka
    ks¾foaY l< yelafla ljrla o?

    1) A only           2) B only           3) C only        4) A and B only 5) A and C only
       A muKs              B muKs              C muKs           A yd B muKs     A yd C muKs

    Answer: 3)

    For an emergency call handling system, minimizing risk, and ensuring no downtime is critical because
    any interruption can result in loss of life or property. Let's review each method.
    yÈis weu;=ï yeisrùfï moaO;shla i|yd" wjodku wju lsÍu iy wl%sh ld,hla fkdue;s nj
    iy;sl lsÍu b;d jeo.;a fõ" ukao ´kEu ndOdjlska Ôú; fyda foam< wysñ úh yelsh' tla tla
    l%uh iudf,dapkh lruq'

    A. Direct Implementation / f¾Çh
    This involves switching from the old system to the new system instantly. This is not recommended for
    emergency systems because if the new system fails, there is no backup.
    fuhg merKs moaO;sfhka kj moaO;shg laIKslj udre ùu we;=<;a fõ' kj moaO;sh wiu;a
    jqjfyd;a Wmia:hla fkdue;s neúka yÈis moaO;s i|yd fuh ks¾foaY fkdlrhs'

    B. Phase Implementation / l,d
    This means introducing the new system in stages. While this can work for some business applications, in
    emergency systems, partial implementation can lead to confusion and gaps in service, as both systems
    may not be fully functional for all scenarios.
    fuhska woyia lrkafka kj moaO;sh wÈhr jYfhka y÷kajd §uhs' fuh iuyr jHdmdßl fhÿï
    i|yd l%shd l< yels jqjo" yÈis moaO;sj," w¾O jYfhka l%shd;aul lsÍu jHdl+,;ajhg iy fiajdfõ
    ysveiaj,g fya;= úh yel" ukao moaO;s folu ish¨ wjia:d i|yd iïmQ¾Kfhkau l%shdldÍ fkdúh
    yelsh'

    C. Parallel Implementation / iudka;r
    This involves running both the old and the new systems simultaneously for a period. This is the safest
    approach for critical systems like emergency call handling because it allows for thorough testing and
    fallback if the new system encounters issues.
    fuhg merKs iy kj moaO;s folu tljr hï ld,hla i|yd l%shd;aul lsÍu we;=<;a fõ' yÈis
    weu;=ï yeisrùu jeks ;SrKd;aul moaO;s i|yd fuh wdrlaIs;u m%fõYhhs" ukao th kj moaO;shg
    .eg¿ we;s jqjfyd;a iïmQ¾K mÍlaIK iy wdmiq yerùug bv i,ihs'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -26-                                          #ictfromabc


    Only parallel implementation (C) is recommended for implementing an emergency call handling system.
    Accordingly, only C is correct.
    yÈis weu;=ï yeisrùfï moaO;shla l%shd;aul lsÍu i|yd iudka;r l%shd;aul lsÍu (C) muKla
    ks¾foaY flf¾' tA wkqj C muKla ksjerÈ fõ'

    Therefore, option 3) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 3) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 22. In a public key cryptographic system, the private key of a person x is given by the function priv(x)
     and the public key is given by the function pub(x).
     fmdÿ h;=re .=ma; fla;l moaO;shl x kï mqoa.,hl=f.a fm!oa.,sl h;=r (private key) priv(x) hk
     Y%s;h u.ska yd fmdÿ h;=r (public key) pub(x) hk Y%s;h u.ska fokq ,efí'
    Consider the following statements:
    my; i|yka j.ka;s i,lkak(
    A. priv(x) and pub(x) should be the same for better security.
       jvd fyd| wdrlaIdjla i|yd priv(x) yd pub(x) hk fol u tlla úh hq;= h'
    B. A message encrypted using pub(x) can be decrypted using pub(x).
       pub(x) Ndú; lr fla;khlrk ,o mKsjqvhla pub(x) Ndú; lr úfla;kh l< yels h'
    C. The person x knows both priv(x) and pub(x).
       x kï mqoa.,hd priv(x) yd pub(x) hk fol u oek isà'
    Which of the above statements is/are correct?
    by; j.ka;s w;=frka ksjerÈ jkafka"

    1) A only           2) C only           3) A and B only 4) A and C only 5) B and C only
       A muKs              C muKs              A yd B muKs     A yd C muKs     B yd C muKs

    Answer: 2)

    Cryptography / .=ma;fla;kh
    Cryptography is the process of hiding or coding information so that only the person a message was
    intended for can read it.
    .=ma;fla;kh hkq mKsúvhla woyia lrk mqoa.,hdg muKla th lshúh yels jk f,i f;dr;=re
    ieÕùu fyda fla;kh lsÍfï l%shdj,shhs'

    Encryption is the process of converting plaintext (readable data) into ciphertext (encoded data) to protect
    it from unauthorized access.
    fla;kh hkq ir, fm< ^lshúh yels o;a;& wkjir m%fõYfhka wdrlaId lsÍu i|yd fla;s; fm<
    ^fla;.; o;a;& njg mßj¾;kh lsÍfï l%shdj,shhs'

    Decryption is the reverse process, converting ciphertext back into plaintext so that authorized users can
    read it.
    úfla;kh hkq tys m%;sf,dau l%shdj,sh jk w;r" n,h,;a mßYS,lhskag lshúh yels mßÈ fla;s; fm<
    kej; idudkH fm< njg mßj¾;kh lrhs'

    Cryptographic Keys / .=ma;fla;k h;=re
    Keys are secret pieces of information used in encryption and decryption. The security of the encrypted
    data depends on keeping the key confidential.
    h;=re hkq fla;kh iy úfla;kh lsÍfï§ Ndú;d lrk ryia f;dr;=re fldgia fõ' fla;d;aul o;a;j,
    wdrlaIdj r|d mj;skafka h;=r ryis.;j ;nd .ekSu u;h'

    Encryption types
    1. Symmetric key encryption (private key encryption)
    iuñ;sl h;=re fla;kh ^mqoa.,sl h;=re fla;kh&

    2. Asymmetric key encryption (Public key encryption)
    wiuñ;sl h;=re fla;kh ^fmdÿ h;=re fla;kh&
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                              -27-                                          #ictfromabc


    Symmetric key encryption / iuñ;sl h;=re fla;kh
    Symmetric key encryption, also known as private-key encryption, uses the same key for both encryption
    and decryption.
    mqoa.,sl h;=re fla;kh f,io ye¢kafjk iuñ;sl h;=re fla;kh" fla;kh iy úfla;kh hk
    folgu tlu h;=r Ndú;d lrhs'

    This means that the same key is used to encode the plaintext into ciphertext and to decode it back to
    plaintext.
    fuhska woyia lrkafka ir, fm, fla;kh lsÍug iy th kej; ir, fm<g úfla;kh lsÍug tlu
    h;=r Ndú;d lrk njhs'

    Asymmetric Key Encryption / wiuñ;sl h;=re fla;kh
    Asymmetric key encryption, also known as public-key encryption, uses a pair of keys
    fmdÿ h;=re fla;kh f,io ye¢kafjk wiuñ;sl h;=re fla;kh u.ska h;=re hq.,hla Ndú;d lrhs

    A public key and a private key.
    fmdÿ h;=rla iy mqoa.,sl h;=rla

    Each participant in a communication has their own unique pair of these keys.
    ikaksfõokhl isák iEu md¾Yjhlgu fuu h;=re hq.,hla we;'

    These keys are mathematically related, meaning that the encryption performed with one key can only be
    decrypted with the corresponding key.
    fuu h;=re .Ks;uh jYfhka iïnkaO fõ" tkï tla h;=rlska isÿ lrk fla;kh úfla;kh l<
    yelafla thg wod< wfkla h;=frka muKs'

    If information is encrypted using the public key, only the corresponding private key can decrypt it and
    vice versa.
    fmdÿ h;=r Ndú;fhka f;dr;=re fla;kh lr we;akï" th úfla;kh l< yelafla thg wod< mqoa.,sl
    h;=rg muKs' th wfkla w;go isÿ l< yel'

    Public Key
    Can be shared openly and is used to encrypt data or verify signatures.
    újD;j fnod .; yels w;r o;a; fla;kh lsÍug fyda w;aika i;Hdmkh lsÍug Ndú;d lrhs'

    Private Key
    Must be kept secret and secure; it is used to decrypt data or create signatures.
    ryis.;j iy wdrlaIs;j ;nd .; hq;=h' th o;a; úfla;kh lsÍug fyda w;aika iE§ug Ndú;d lrhs

    Let's analyze each statement one by one. / wms tla tla m%ldYh tlska tl úYaf,aIKh lruq'

    Statement / m%ldYh A)
    Incorrect / jerÈ
    In public key cryptography, priv(x) (private key) and pub(x) (public key) must be different.
    fmdÿ h;=re .=ma; fla;kfha§" priv(x) ^mqoa.,sl h;=r& iy pub(x) ^fmdÿ h;=r& fjkia úh hq;=h'

    Security relies on them being mathematically linked but not identical.
    wdrlaIdj r|d mj;skafka tAjd .Ks;uh jYfhka iïnkaO ù ;sîu u; kuq;a iudk fkdfõ'

    The public key is shared with everyone, and the private key is kept secret.
    fmdÿ h;=r iEu flfkl= iuÕu fnodf.k we;s w;r mqoa.,sl h;=r ryis.;j ;nd we;'

    If they were the same, the system would not be asymmetric, which is essential for public key cryptography.
    tAjd iudk kï" moaO;sh wiuñ;sl fkdjkq we;" th fmdÿ h;=re .=ma; fla;kh i|yd w;HjYH fõ'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                  -28-                                            #ictfromabc


    Statement / m%ldYh B)
    Incorrect / jerÈ
    This describes symmetric encryption, not asymmetric.
    fuh wiuñ;sl fkdj iuñ;sl fla;kh úia;r lrhs'

    In public key systems / fmdÿ h;=re moaO;sj,
    A message encrypted with pub(x) is decrypted with priv(x).
    Pub(x) iuÕ fla;kh lrk ,o mKsúvhla priv(x) iuÕ úfla;kh lr we;'

    A message encrypted with priv(x) (e.g., for digital signatures) is decrypted with pub(x).
    Priv(x) iuÕ fla;kh lrk ,o mKsúvhla ^Wod( äðg,a w;aika i|yd& pub(x) iuÕ úfla;kh lr
    we;'

    So, pub(x) cannot decrypt a message it encrypted.
    tneúka" pub(x) yg th fla;kh l< mKsúvhla úfla;kh l< fkdyel'

    Statement / m%ldYh C)
    Correct / ksjerÈ
    The person x generates the key pair.
    x mqoa.,hd h;=re hq.,h ckkh lrhs'

    They keep priv(x) private and share pub(x) with others.
    Tjqka priv(x) mqoa.,slj ;nd .kakd w;r pub(x) wka wh iuÕ fnod .kS'

    So yes, x knows both keys.
    Tõ" x h;=re folu okS'

    Therefore, option 2) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 2) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 23. Local Area Network (LAN) has 500 network devices. What is the most appropriate subnet mask for
     this computer network?
     ia:dkSh fmfoia cd,hl (LAN) cd, Wml%u (network devices) 500 la ;sfí' tu mß.Kl cd,h i|yd
     jvd;a u iqÿiq Wmcd, wdjrKh (subnet mask) l=ula o

    1) 255.255.255.0                   2) 255.255.255.128                 3) 255.255.255.192
    4) 255.255.255.224                 5) 255.255.254.0

    Answer: 5)

    A LAN with 500 network devices requires at least 500 usable IP addresses. Let's analyze each subnet mask
    option:
    cd, Wmdx. 500la iys; LAN tllg wju jYfhka Ndú;d l< yels IP ,smsk 500la wjYH fõ' tla
    tla Wmcd, wdjrK úl,amh úYaf,aIKh lruq(

    Understanding Subnet Masks and Usable Ips / Wmcd, wdjrK iy Ndú;d l< yels IP f;areï .ekSu
    A subnet mask determines the network portion and the host portion of an IP address. The number of usable
    IP addresses in a subnet is calculated as 2h−2, where 'h' is the number of host bits (bits set to 0 in the subnet
    mask). We subtract 2 because the first address is the network address and the last is the broadcast address,
    neither of which can be assigned to a device.
    Wmcd, wdjrKhla IP ,smskhl cd, fldgi iy Odrl fldgi ;SrKh lrhs' Wmcd,hl Ndú;d l<
    yels IP ,smsk .Kk 2h−2 f,i .Kkh flf¾" tys§ h hkq Odrl ìgq .Kkhs ^Wmcd, wdjrKfha ìgq
    0 f,i ilid we;&' m<uq ,smskh cd, ,smskh jk w;r wjidk ,smskh úldYk ,smskh jk neúka wms
    2 wvq lruq" tA foflka tllaj;a Wmdx.hlg mejßh fkdyel'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                             -29-                                        #ictfromabc


    Option/ úl,amh 1)
    255.255.255.0
    In binary, this is 11111111.11111111.11111111.00000000.

    Number of host bits (h) = 8
    Odrl ìgq .Kk (h) = 8

    Usable IP addresses / Ndú;d l< yels IP ,smsk = 28 – 2 = 256 – 2 = 254.

    This is not enough for 500 devices.
    fuh Wmdx. 500la i|yd m%udKj;a fkdfõ'

    Option/ úl,amh 2)
    255.255.255.128
    In binary, this is 11111111.11111111.11111111.10000000.

    Number of host bits (h) = 7
    Odrl ìgq .Kk (h) = 7

    Usable IP addresses / Ndú;d l< yels IP ,smsk = 27 −2 = 128 – 2 = 126.

    This is not enough for 500 devices.
    fuh Wmdx. 500la i|yd m%udKj;a fkdfõ'

    Option/ úl,amh 3)
    255.255.255.192
    In binary, this is 11111111.11111111.11111111.11000000.

    Number of host bits (h) = 6
    Odrl ìgq .Kk (h) = 6

    Usable IP addresses / Ndú;d l< yels IP ,smsk = 26 – 2 = 64 – 2 = 62.

    This is not enough for 500 devices.
    fuh Wmdx. 500la i|yd m%udKj;a fkdfõ'

    Option/ úl,amh 4)
    255.255.255.224
    In binary, this is 11111111.11111111.11111111.11100000.

    Number of host bits (h) = 5
    Odrl ìgq .Kk (h) = 5

    Usable IP addresses / Ndú;d l< yels IP ,smsk = 25 – 2 = 32 – 2 = 30.

    This is not enough for 500 devices.
    fuh Wmdx. 500la i|yd m%udKj;a fkdfõ'

    Option/ úl,amh 5)
    255.255.254.0
    In binary, this is 11111111.11111111.11111110.00000000.

    Number of host bits (h) = 9 (1 bit from the third octet + 8 bits from the fourth octet)
    Odrl ìgq .Kk (h) = 9 ^;=kajk wIaGufhka ìgq 1la + y;rjk wIaGufhka ìgq 8la&

    Usable IP addresses / Ndú;d l< yels IP ,smsk = 29 – 2 = 512 – 2 = 510.
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                 -30-                                             #ictfromabc


    This is sufficient for 500 devices.
    fuh Wmdx. 500 lg m%udKj;a fõ'

    Therefore, option 5) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 5) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 24. Consider the following statements about compilers and interpreters used in programming languages:
     l%uf,aLk NdIdjkays Ndú; jk iïmdol yd w¾: úkHdil (compilers and interpreters) iïnkaOfhka
     my; oelafjk j.ka;s i,lkak(
    A. Compilers/interpreters are not required to execute a program written in assembly language.
       Assembly NdIdfjka ,shd we;s l%uf,aLhla l%shd;aul lsÍu i|yd iïmdol fyda w¾: úkHdil
       wjYH fkdfõ'
    B. Compilers are not essential to execute a program in machine code.
       hka;% fla;j,ska we;s l%uf,aLhla l%shd;aul lsÍu i|yd iïmdol w;HjYH fkdfõ'
    C. An executable program is translated into a source program by a compiler.
       l%shd;aul l< yels (executable) l%uf,aLhla iïmdolhla u.ska uQ, l%uf,aLhla (source program)
       njg mßj¾;kh lrkq ,nhs'
    Which of the above statements is/are correct?
    by; j.ka;s w;=frka ksjerÈ jkafka"

    1) A only            2) B only           3) C only            4) A and B only 5) B and C only
       A muKs               B muKs              C muKs               A yd B muKs     B yd C muKs

    Answer: 2)

    Explanation /meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    Statement / m%ldYh A)
    This statement is incorrect. Assembly language requires an assembler, which is a type of translator, to
    convert the assembly code into machine code for execution. While compilers or interpreters are not
    directly involved, a translation step is still necessary. Hence, the assertion that no translation is required is
    false.
    fuu m%ldYh ksjerÈ fkdfõ' l%shd;aul lsÍu i|yd Assembly fla;h hka;% fla;hla njg mßj¾;kh
    lsÍu i|yd Assembly NdIdjg assembler lrkafkl= wjYH fõ" th mßj¾;l j¾.hls' iïmdolhska
    fyda mßj¾;lhska iDcqju iïnkaO fkdjQj;a" mßj¾;k mshjrla ;ju;a wjYH fõ' tneúka"
    mßj¾;khla wjYH fkdjk njg lrk m%ldYh wi;H fõ'

    Statement / m%ldYh B)
    This statement is correct. Machine code is already in a format that can be directly executed by the
    processor, without the need for a compiler. Compilers are used to convert high-level languages into
    machine code, but once in machine code form, a compiler is not needed for execution.
    fuu m%ldYh ksjerÈ fõ' hdka;%sl fla;h oekgu;a iïmdolfhl=f.a wjYH;djhlska f;drj ilikh
    uÕska iDcqju l%shd;aul l< yels wdlD;shlska mj;S' by< uÜgfï NdId hka;% fla;hla njg
    mßj¾;kh lsÍu i|yd iïmdol Ndú;d lrkq ,efí" kuq;a hka;% fla; wdldrfhka .;a miq" l%shd;aul
    lsÍu i|yd iïmdolhla wjYH fkdfõ'

    Statement / m%ldYh C)
    This statement is incorrect. The role of a compiler is to translate source code (written in a high-level
    programming language) into an executable program, not the other way around. This reversal of the
    compiler's function makes the statement false.
    fuu m%ldYh ksjerÈ fkdfõ' iïmdolfhl=f.a ld¾hNdrh jkafka ^by< uÜgfï l%uf,aLk NdIdjlska
    ,shd we;s& m%Nj fla;h l%shd;aul l< yels jevigykla njg mßj¾;kh lsÍuhs" wfkla w;g fkdfõ'
    iïmdolhdf.a Y%s;fha fuu m%;sj¾;kh m%ldYh wi;H lrhs'

    Therefore, option 2) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 2) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

     B kï mß.Klfhys Odjkh jk fjí fiajdodhlfhys mj;sk fjí msgqjla" A kï fiajd.%dyl
     mß.Klfhys l%shd;aul jk fjí w;ßlaiqjla u.ska úoeyq (rendering) lrhs' my; i|yka ljrla

    1)   Size of images in the web page / fjí msgqfõ we;s wkqrEmj, (image) m%udKh
    2)   Number of colours in the web page / fjí msgqfõ we;s j¾K ixLHdj
    3)   Speed of the client computer / fiajd.%dyl mß.Klfha fõ.h
    4)   Efficiency of the web browser software / fjí w;ßlaiq uDÿldx.fha ld¾hlaIu;dj

    Option/ úl,amh 1)

    jerÈhs' úYd, rEm mQrKh ùug iy úoeyqï lsÍug jeä ld,hla .;fõ" úfYaIfhka ukao.dó cd,
    iïnkaO;d j,§' tAjd jeä l,dm m<,la iy ieliqï ld,hla jeh lrk w;r" th mßYS,lhdg

    This is correct because the number of colours used in a web page does not affect how fast the page is
    rendered. Modern web browsers and display systems are optimized to handle a wide range of colours
    efficiently. Whether a page uses a few colours or many, it does not slow down the browser’s rendering
    process in any noticeable way.

    jerÈhs' fiajdodhl hka;%fha ^CPU, RAM wdÈh& l%shdldß;ajh n%õirhg wka;¾.;h w¾: ksrEmKh
    lr m%o¾Ykh l< yels wdldrh fl,skau n,mdhs' ukao.dó mß.Kl tlu msgqj úoeyqï lsÍug jeä
    ld,hla .;fõ'

    jerÈhs' úúO n%õi¾ úúO úoeyqïlrK tkaðka Ndú;d lrhs' iuyr tAjd wfkla tAjdg jvd
    m%Yia;slrKh lr we;' fyd¢ka m%Yia;slrKh l< n%õirhlg wvq ld¾hlaIu tllg jvd fõ.fhka

 26. Consider the following statements about Dynamic Random Access Memory (DRAM)
     .;sl iiïNdù m%fõY u;lh (DRAM) iïnkaOfhka my; § we;s j.ka;s i,lkak(
    A. Periodic refreshing is required for DRAM.
       DRAM i|yd ld,dj¾;l mqnqÿ lsÍula (periodic refreshing) wjYH fõ'
    B. Registers in the processor are made of DRAMs.
       ilikfha we;s frðia;r DRAM j,ska ksmojd we;'
    C. Memory density of DRAM is higher than that of static RAM.
       DRAM ys u;l >kkajh iaÓ;sl RAM ys u;l >k;ajhg jvd jeä h'
    Which of the above statements is/are correct?
    by; j.ka;s w;=frka ksjerÈ jkafka ljrla o?

    1) A only           2) B only           3) A and B only 4) A and C only 5) B and C only
       A muKs              B muKs              A yd B muKs     A yd C muKs     B yd C muKs

    Answer: 4)

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    Statement / m%ldYh A)
    DRAM stores each bit of data in a capacitor, which gradually loses charge over time. Because of this,
    DRAM must be refreshed periodically to retain the data, typically every few milliseconds. This refresh
    process is what makes DRAM dynamic, in contrast to static RAM, which doesn’t need refreshing.
    Although this adds complexity, DRAM remains popular due to its high capacity and low cost.
    DRAM uÕska iEu o;a; fldgilau Odß;%lhl .nvd lrk w;r" th ld,h;a iuÕ wdfrdamKh
    l%ufhka wvq fõ' fï ksid" o;a; r|jd .ekSu i|yd DRAM jßka jr kejqï l< hq;=h" idudkHfhka
    iEu ñ,s ;;amr lsysmhlg jrla th l, hq;=h' kejqï lsÍu wjYH fkdjk iaÓ;sl RAM j,g
    fjkiaj" fuu kejqï lsÍfï l%shdj,sh DRAM .;sl lrhs' fuh ixlS¾K;djhla tla l<o" DRAM
    tys by< Odß;dj iy wvq msßjeh fya;=fjka ckm%shj mj;S'

    Statement / m%ldYh B)
    Processor registers are built using Static RAM (SRAM) or flip-flop circuits, not DRAM. Registers
    require very high speed and instant access, which DRAM cannot provide due to its need for refreshing
    and slower access times. DRAM is used in main memory (RAM), not in CPU registers.
    ilik frðiag¾ DRAM fkdj iaÓ;sl RAM SRAM fyda *a,sma-*af,dma mßm: Ndú;fhka f.dvk.d
    we;' frðiag¾ j,g b;d by< fõ.hla iy laIKsl m%fõYhla wjYH jk w;r" th kejqï lsÍfï
    wjYH;djh iy ukao.dó m%fõY ld,h ksid DRAM yg ,nd Èh fkdyel' CPU frðiag¾ j, fkdj
    m%Odk u;lfha (RAM) DRAM Ndú;d fõ'

    Statement / m%ldYh C)
    DRAM cells are smaller and simpler (one transistor and one capacitor per bit), which allows more
    memory cells to be packed into the same physical area. This results in higher memory density
    compared to Static RAM (SRAM), which uses multiple transistors per bit. Because of this, DRAM is
    more cost-effective for creating large-capacity memory modules. That’s why DRAM is used in main
    system memory while SRAM is used for cache.
    DRAM ieÿï tAllh l=vd iy ir, fõ ^ìÜ tllg g%dkaisiagrhla iy Odß;%lhla&" tuÕska jeä
    u;l ffi, tlu fN!;sl m%foaYhg weiqreï lsÍug bv i,ihs' fuh ìÜ tllg nyq g%dkaisiagr
    Ndú;d lrk iaÓ;sl RAM SRAM yd ii|k úg by< u;l >k;ajhla we;s lrhs' fï ksid" úYd,
    Odß;djlska hq;a u;l fudähq, ks¾udKh lsÍu i|yd DRAM jvd ,dNodhS fõ' tA ksid m%Odk moaO;s
    u;lfha DRAM Ndú;d lrk w;r SRAM ksys; u;lh i|yd Ndú;d lrhs'

    Therefore, option 4) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 4) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 28. Consider the following statements about flowcharts:
     .e,Sï igyka iïnkaOfhka my; j.ka;s i,ld n,kak(
    A. A flowchart is a pictorial representation of an algorithm.
       .e,Sï igykla hkq we,af.daß;uhl Ñ;%l ksrEmKhla fõ'
    B. A flowchart may have more than one ‘stop’ or ‘end’ termination symbols.
       .e,Sï igykl 'kej;Su' fyda 'wjidkh' kï jQ wjika lsÍfï ixfla; tllg jvd mej;sh yels h'
    C. Algorithms can be represented only by using flowcharts.
       we,af.daß;u ksrEmKh l< yels jkafka .e,Sï igyka Ndú;fhka muKs'
    Which of the above statements is/are correct?
    by; j.ka;s w;=frka ksjerÈ jkafka"

    1) A only           2) B only           3) C only           4) A and B only 5) B and C only
       A muKs              B muKs              C muKs              A yd B muKs     B yd C muKs

    Answer: 4)

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    Statement / m%ldYh A)
    This statement is correct. Flowcharts use symbols such as ovals, rectangles, and arrows to visually depict
    the sequence of steps in an algorithm. This graphical representation helps in understanding the logic and
    structure of the algorithm.
    fuu m%ldYh ksjerÈ fõ' we,af.dß;uhl mshjr wkqms<sfj, oDYHuh jYfhka ksrEmKh lsÍu i|yd
    .e,Sï igyka ´j,dldr" iDcqfldaKdi% iy B;, jeks ixfla; Ndú;d lrhs' fuu Ñ;%l ksrEmKh
    we,af.dß;ufha ;¾lkh iy jHqyh f;areï .ekSug WmldÍ fõ'

    Statement / m%ldYh B)
    This statement is correct. A flowchart can have multiple end points depending on the logic of the algorithm.
    For instance, in a decision-based process, different conditions may lead to separate termination points,
    making it valid to have more than one ‘stop’ symbol.
    fuu m%ldYh ksjerÈ fõ' we,af.dß;ufha ;¾lkh wkqj .e,Sï igyklg nyq wka; ,laIH ;sìh
    yelsh' WodyrKhla f,i" ;SrK u; mokï jQ l%shdj,shl§" úúO ;;ajhka fjku wjika ,laIHj,g
    fya;= úh yels w;r" th ’stop’ ixfla; tllg jvd ;sîu j,x.= lrhs'

    Statement / m%ldYh C)
    This statement is incorrect. Algorithms can be represented in multiple ways, including pseudocode, plain
    text, and programming languages, in addition to flowcharts. Flowcharts are one of many tools for
    representing algorithms but not the only method.

    1) i = 0 and i ≤ 5                        2) i = 1 and i = 5                3) i = 0 and i > 5
    4) i = 1 and i ≤ 5                        5) i = 1 and i ≥ 5

    Answer: 4)
    The flowchart's logic involves initializing a counter variable `i` to begin at 1 and then iterating through a
    loop five times while checking if the condition `i <= 5` holds true. This ensures that the program reads
    five values, adds them to the total (`tot`), and increments `i` in each iteration. The condition `i <= 5`
    ensures the loop terminates once `i` exceeds 5, maintaining proper logic and preventing infinite loops.
    This logic is standard in Python and works identically in both Python 2 and Python 3.

    difference in how Python 2 and Python 3 interpret them. Both versions treat `i = 1` as an initialization and
    `i <= 5` as a valid comparison. The flowchart’s logic aligns with fundamental programming principles,
    making the answer common across Python versions. Therefore, the correct choices for `A` and `B` are `i

 31. Consider the following Python statement:
     my; i|yka mhs;ka j.ka;sh i,lkak(

    temp = [23,45,2,-2,0][:2:]

    What would be the value of the variable temp after executing the above statement?
    by; j.ka;sh l%shd;aul jQ miq temp kï jQ úp,Hfhys mj;sk w.h l=ula o?

    1) 23,45            2) [23,45]          3) 23,2             4) [23,2]            5) [23,2,0]

    Answer: 2)

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    Python Statement / mhs;ka m%ldYkh
    temp = [23, 45, 2, -2, 0][:2:]
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                  -38-                                             #ictfromabc


    According to Python 2 / Python 2 j,g wkqj
    In Python 2, the syntax for slicing a list is the same as in the current version. The list `[23, 45, 2, -2, 0]` is
    sliced using `[:2:]`. This slicing takes elements starting from the beginning (`:`) up to (but not including)
    index `2` (`2:`). Therefore, only the first two elements `[23, 45]` are selected. The final result stored in
    `temp` is `[23, 45]`, which is a list.
    Python 2 ys" ,ehsia;=jla slicing lsÍu i|yd jk ldrl Í;sh jkafka oekg mj;sk mhs;kayS we;s
    wdldrhgu fõ' [23, 45, 2, -2, 0] ,ehsia;=j [:2:] Ndú;fhka slice lr we;' fuu slicing lsÍu wdrïNfha
    (`:`) isg ^kuq;a we;=<;a fkdlr& `2` (`2:`) o¾Ylh olajd uQ,dx. .kS' tneúka" [23, 45] m<uq uQ,dx.
    fol muKla f;dard .kq ,efí' `temp` ys .nvd lr we;s wjidk m%;sM,h [23, 45] jk w;r th
    ,ehsia;=jls'

    According to Current Python / j;auka Python j,g wkqj
    The slicing behavior remains identical in the current version of Python. The statement `[:2:]` extracts
    elements from the beginning up to index `2`, and `[23, 45]` is stored in `temp`. The result is still a list and
    remains `[23, 45]`.
    Python ys j;auka wkqjdofha slicing lsÍfï yeisÍu iudkj mj;S' [:2:] m%ldYh wdrïNfha isg `2`
    o¾Ylh olajd uQ,dx. Wmqgd .kakd w;r [23, 45] `temp` ys .nvd lr we;' m%;sM,h ;ju;a ,ehsia;=jla
    jk w;r [23, 45] f,i mj;S'

    Therefore, option 2) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 2) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

    taken. It is easily accessible and intended for short-term manual handling.

 34. Which of the following high-level data flow diagrams is correct with respect to the rules on data flow
     modelling?
     my; olajd we;s by< uÜgfï o;a; .e,Sï igyka w;=frka o;a; .e,Sï wdlD;slrK kS;sj,g wkql+,
     j ksjeros jkafka l=ula o?
                                   1
    1)       A                                                 M1      C
                                        B


                                   1
    2)       A                                                 M1      C
                                        B


                                   1
    3)       A                                                 M1      C
                                        B


                                   1
    4)       A                                                 M1      C
                                        B


                                   1
    5)       A                                                 M1      C
                                        B


    Answer: 5)

    To determine the correct high-level data flow diagram with respect to the rules on data flow modeling, we
    need to consider standard conventions. In data flow diagrams (DFDs), the following rules typically apply.
    o;a; m%jdy wdlD;slrKh ms<sn| kS;sÍ;sj,g wod<j ksjerÈ by< uÜgfï o;a; m%jdy rEm igyk
    ;SrKh lsÍu i|yd" wms iïu; iïuq;Ska i,ld ne,sh hq;=h' o;a; m%jdy rEm igyka (DFDs) j,§"
    my; i|yka kS;s idudkHfhka wod< fõ'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -41-                                          #ictfromabc


    •   Processes (e.g., B) are represented by rectangles and transform incoming data flows into outgoing data
        flows.
        l%shdj,s (Wod( B) iDcqfldaKdi% u.ska ksrEmKh jk w;r tk o;a; m%jdy msg;g hk o;a; m%jdy
        njg mßj¾;kh fõ'
    •   Data stores (e.g., the box with "1" inside) hold data and are connected to processes with two-way
        arrows if data is both read from and written to the store.
        o;a; .nvd (Wod: "1" we;=<; we;s fldgqj) o;a; r|jd ;nd .kakd w;r o;a; .nvdfjka lshjd
        ,shd we;akï oaú-ud¾. B;, iys; l%shdj,Skag iïnkaO fõ'
    •   External entities (e.g., A and C) are represented by ovals and interact with the system by sending or
        receiving data.
        ndysr wdh;k (Wod( A iy C) ´j,dldr u.ska ksrEmKh jk w;r o;a; heùfuka fyda ,eîfuka
        moaO;sh iuÕ wka;¾ l%shd lrhs'
    •   Data flows are represented by arrows, showing the direction of data movement.
        o;a; m%jdy B;, u.ska ksrEmKh jk w;r o;a; p,kfha ÈYdj fmkajhs'
    •   A process should have at least one input and one output data flow.
        l%shdj,shlg wju jYfhka tla wdodkhla iy tla m%;sodk o;a; m%jdyhla ;sìh hq;=h'

    Analyzing the diagrams / rEm igyka úYaf,aIKh lsÍu

    Option/ úl,amh 1)
    In (1), the data store has an input from A but no output to B, which is incorrect.
    1 ys" o;a; .nvdjg A fj;ska wdodkhla we;s kuq;a B fj; m%;sodkhla fkdue;" th jerÈh'

     jkafka my; i|yka l=ula o?

    using parentheses without quotes. `d3` is a list because it is enclosed in square brackets (`[1,2,(1,2)]`), and
    the third element of the list is a tuple.
    `d1` hkq WoaOD; ,l=Kq (`"(1,2,3)"`) ;=< oud we;s neúka th string tlls' `d2` hkq WoaOD; ,l=Kq
    fkdue;sj jryka Ndú;fhka w¾: olajd we;s neúka th gm,a tlls' `d3` hkq y;/ia jryka

    According to the current Python's syntax rules / j;auka mhs;ka ldrl Í;s j,g wkqj
    The behavior is the same as in Python 2. `d1` is a string, `d2` is a tuple, and `d3` is a list containing a tuple
    as its third element.
    yeisÍu mhs;ka 2 ys fuka u fõ' `d1` hkq string tlls" `d2` hkq gm,a tlla jk w;r `d3` hkq tys
